class="label">Share</div></button></div></div></div></div><div class="visibility-check"></div><div><div><div class="available-content"><div dir="auto" class="body markup"><p>When it comes to communicating with investors, public companies have a powerful tool at their disposal: the shareholder letter. Often included in an annual report—but increasingly distributed quarterly—a shareholder letter isn’t just a summary of financial results. It's a strategic narrative that can build trust, align expectations, and even reinforce a company’s brand. </p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" x2="10" y1="21" y2="14"></line></svg></button></div></div></div></a></figure></div><h2 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>What Is a Shareholder Letter?</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§what-is-a-shareholder-letter" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/what-is-a-shareholder-letter" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h2><p>A shareholder letter is a direct communication from a company’s leadership—usually the CEO or chairman—to its shareholders. The primary purpose is to provide an overview of the company’s performance over the past quarter or year. But beyond the numbers, these letters serve as a platform for leaders to articulate their vision, explain strategic decisions, and build a narrative that resonates with investors.</p><p>Historically, shareholder letters were simple recaps of financial performance. But as the role of investor relations has evolved, so too have shareholder letters. They have become a key component within the investor marketing toolkit.</p><h2 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>How Shareholder Letters Support Investor Marketing Goals</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§how-shareholder-letters-support-investor-marketing-goals" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/how-shareholder-letters-support-investor-marketing-goals" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h2><p>Investor marketing is all about building a strong brand around your company and stock ticker. Modern investors expect more frequent communication, in formats they find accessible and easy to digest. They are also looking for strong signals of competent and trustworthy leadership—and these letters are one of the things investors look at to read between the lines.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" href="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!kJy6!,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fab475547-6c18-4c2c-a48a-b6c8723f4e65_1600x900.png" href="https://www.marketwatch.com/story/how-sharp-investors-use-a-ceos-annual-shareholder-letter-as-a-secret-door-to-superior-stocks-2020-11-06">Image via MarketWatch</a></figcaption></figure></div><p>A well-crafted shareholder letter should do three things:</p><ul><li><p><strong>Educate Investors: </strong><span>Clearly explain the company’s performance, strategy, and market conditions in a way that’s accessible to all investors, regardless of their financial expertise. </span></p></li><li><p><strong>Build Trust: </strong><span>Be candid about successes and challenges. Transparency fosters trust, and trust is crucial for maintaining investor confidence. Effective shareholder letters demonstrate thought leadership in the category and a confidence in leadership to meet its stated objectives.</span></p></li><li><p><strong>Align Expectations: </strong><span>Set realistic expectations for the future by clearly outlining the company’s long-term strategy and goals.</span></p></li></ul><h2 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>Key Elements of an Effective Shareholder Letter</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§key-elements-of-an-effective-shareholder-letter" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/key-elements-of-an-effective-shareholder-letter" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h2><p>Shareholder letters vary by company and executives; however, there are common attributes that many of them follow to meet their stated objectives. These aren’t just best practices—they’re must-haves for making a letter that truly connects with investors and boosts your ticker brand.</p><h3 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>1. Clarity and simplicity</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§1-clarity-and-simplicity" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/1-clarity-and-simplicity" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h3><p>The best shareholder letters are easy to read. They avoid jargon and complex language. Instead, they focus on clear, direct communication. The goal is to make sure every investor understands the company’s performance and strategy without needing a financial dictionary.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" href="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!NltH!,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F422966f2-8905-4b54-a8fe-e7fac30ca565_606x786.png" company’s mission and focus.</span></figcaption></figure></div><h3 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>2. Transparency and candor</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§2-transparency-and-candor" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/2-transparency-and-candor" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h3><p>Investors appreciate honesty. If the company faced challenges during the year, don’t gloss over them. Address them directly, explain the steps being taken to overcome them, and be transparent about what this means for the future. This honesty not only builds credibility but also sets realistic expectations for the future.</p><p>Consider this example from Block CEO Jack Dorsey, which walks investors through with candid remarks on the parts of the company’s he and the team are focused on “fixing.”</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" href="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!ANfM!,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Feb17db11-0264-4f92-8d3f-596b59e31aaa_1210x960.png" class="header-anchor-post"><strong>3. Engagement through storytelling</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§3-engagement-through-storytelling" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/3-engagement-through-storytelling" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h3><p>A shareholder letter should tell a story. This could be the story of the company’s growth, a reflection on the year’s major events, or an insight into the company’s long-term vision. Storytelling makes the letter more engaging and helps investors connect with the company on a more personal level. Warren Buffett’s letters to Berkshire Hathaway shareholders are a prime example—they are as famous for their folksy, engaging style as they are for their financial insights.</p><p>We’re seeing companies utilize the real estate afforded by shareholder letters to add additional layers of storytelling, including social proof from customer testimonials and media coverage. </p><p>Consider this example from Block, which pulls publicly-shared tweets from customers within its letter.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" href="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!mnxs!,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Fa6c701ce-0d09-4412-a986-3edff9f1a192_1280x732.png" testimonials</span></figcaption></figure></div><p>Rivian has included press quotes in its shareholder newsletter. This is effective since shareholders may not see every headline on their own. In aggregate, the media recap spotlights public reception and signals company momentum.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" In this example from Zillow, the company’s co-founder used the letter to introduce a major C-suite change, which included rationale for the decision, background on the hire, and a vote of confidence moving forward.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" href="https://s24.q4cdn.com/723050407/files/doc_earnings/2024/q2/presentation/Zillow-2Q24-Shareholders-Letter.pdf">Zillow Shareholder Letter</a><span> provides context to a key leadership update</span></figcaption></figure></div><h3 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>4. Focus on long-term strategy</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§4-focus-on-long-term-strategy" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/4-focus-on-long-term-strategy" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h3><p>While it’s important to discuss the past quarter’s performance, a shareholder letter should also focus on the future. What is the company’s long-term strategy? How does management plan to achieve it? This forward-looking approach helps to align investor expectations with the company’s goals. </p><p>When talking about key themes that will drive the company in the future, there is no such thing as overemphasizing. These statements should be repeated over and over again so that investors have a clear sense of where you’re going.</p><p>In this example from Rivian, the beginning of the letter opens with a succinct breakdown of the quarter’s highlights and priorities. Using this format, the key points can be digested at-a-glance.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" href="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!DBhH!,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2Faf371c40-a4ee-4637-bd0a-fc855f490903_1216x1478.png" company’s top priorities</span></figcaption></figure></div><h3 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>5. Use of visuals</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§5-use-of-visuals" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/5-use-of-visuals" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h3><p>Incorporating charts, tables, and other visuals can help to break down complex information and make the letter more accessible. Historically, this has meant using data visualization to bring financial statement trends to life. Increasingly, companies are also working in product screenshots and other creative elements that help bring the story to life.</p><p>Personalization is a major focus for Hims &amp; Hers, so the company shows momentum in subscribers utilizing personalized products as a key visual in its quarterly letter.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" class="header-anchor-post"><strong>The Evolving Nature of Shareholder Letters</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§the-evolving-nature-of-shareholder-letters" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/the-evolving-nature-of-shareholder-letters" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h2><p>Over time, the format and content of shareholder letters have evolved. Where once these letters were little more than dry financial recaps, they are now dynamic documents that reflect the company’s brand and values. In the past shareholder letters usually came once per year, but now, many companies are opting to distribute them monthly alongside quarterly earnings reports.</p><h3 class="header-anchor-post"><strong>Improved distribution and accessibility</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§improved-distribution-and-accessibility" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/improved-distribution-and-accessibility" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h3><p><span>Companies are also improving their approach to distribution. While most companies host shareholder letters on their IR website, others push shareholders to sign up for quarterly newsletter alerts direct to their inbox. An example is </span><a href="https://investor.snap.com/overview/default.aspx">Snap</a><span>, which sends out a quarterly investor newsletter inclusive of IR and product updates. </span></p><p><span>In the example below, </span><a href="https://www.siemens.com/global/en/company/investor-relations/investor-services.html">Siemens</a><span> includes a strong call to action to “subscribe” alongside its quarterly letter. </span></p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§brand-alignment-and-design" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/brand-alignment-and-design" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" work for Warren Buffett, but Berkshire Hathaway is the exception and not the rule in this case. In fact, Berkshire’s “anti-design” aesthetic has become its very brand. </p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" Hathaway</a><span>’s repository of past letters</span></figcaption></figure></div><p><span>Most IROs will want to look to companies like </span><a href="https://investors.duolingo.com/">Duolingo</a><span>, which have integrated vibrant visuals and product-related stories into their shareholder letters, making them not only informative but also visually appealing. Other examples include </span><a href="https://downloads.rivian.com/2md5qhoeajym/1TJ6otGQ8dmSyIi0shstum/3f443d04053237a398665ae3dd4a79dd/EX_-_99.2_2Q24_Shareholder_Letter_Final.pdf">Rivian</a><span>, </span><a href="https://s27.q4cdn.com/787306631/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/FINAL-Q2-2024-Shareholder-Letter_Linked-1.pdf">Airbnb</a><span>, </span><a href="https://investors.sonder.com/static-files/414a6101-2aa0-4716-ba03-8c1610606c52">Sonder</a><span>, </span><a href="https://s27.q4cdn.com/787306631/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/FINAL-Q2-2024-Shareholder-Letter_Linked-1.pdf">Hims &amp; Hers</a><span>, and </span><a href="https://s29.q4cdn.com/628966176/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/Block_Shareholder-Letter_2Q24.pdf">Block</a><span>, to name a few.</span></p><p>The big challenge when upleveling the design of your IR materials? Resources.</p><p>IR teams do not typically sit within the same area of the organization as marketing and creative teams do, and often do not have the same access to these resources in their own work streams. But this is all changing, as many companies that have recently gone public are clearly giving IR teams the creative resources they need.</p><p>The example from Airbnb almost looks like a print advertisement, but it’s actually just a well-designed page from a recent shareholder letter.</p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" class="header-anchor-post"><strong>Executive Branding Opportunities</strong><div class="pencraft pc-display-flex pc-alignItems-center pc-position-absolute pc-reset header-anchor-parent"><div class="pencraft pc-display-contents pc-reset pubTheme-yiXxQA"><div id="§executive-branding-opportunities" class="pencraft pc-reset header-anchor offset-top"></div><button tabindex="0" type="button" aria-label="Link" data-href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/i/148006476/executive-branding-opportunities" class="pencraft pc-reset pencraft iconButton-mq_Et5 iconButtonBase-dJGHgN buttonBase-GK1x3M buttonStyle-r7yGCK size_sm-G3LciD priority_secondary-S63h9o"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" class="lucide lucide-link"><path d="M10 13a5 5 0 0 0 7.54.54l3-3a5 5 0 0 0-7.07-7.07l-1.72 1.71"></path><path d="M14 11a5 5 0 0 0-7.54-.54l-3 3a5 5 0 0 0 7.07 7.07l1.71-1.71"></path></svg></button></div></div></h3><p><span>Shareholder letters are a great way for executives to </span><a href="https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/p/c-suite-branding-the-key-to-connecting">show who they are as leaders</a><span>. The tone and delivery of these letters give investors a sense of what the leader values and how they approach their role. It’s not just about company updates—it’s also about letting their personality and leadership style shine through.</span></p><p><span>For example, </span><a href="https://s21.q4cdn.com/198919461/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/Shareholder-Letter-Q2-24-FINAL.pdf">Morningstar’s CEO includes a reading list</a><span> to give shareholders a sense of the topics he’s following, and what’s sparking his curiosity as of late.</span></p><div class="captioned-image-container"><figure><a target="_blank" href="https://substackcdn.com/image/fetch/$s_!6S0G!,f_auto,q_auto:good,fl_progressive:steep/https%3A%2F%2Fsubstack-post-media.s3.amazonaws.com%2Fpublic%2Fimages%2F64695c59-b9d9-4847-9086-25cddf7bef91_1600x1451.png" letter does more than report on a company’s financials—it serves as a critical tool in investor marketing.</strong><span> By focusing on clarity, transparency, and storytelling, companies can create shareholder letters that not only inform but also engage and inspire. As investor expectations continue to evolve, so too must the way companies communicate. </span></p><p>By mastering the art of the shareholder letter, companies can turn a simple communication into a powerful tool for building investor confidence and driving long-term growth.</p><p><strong><a href="https://stakeholderlabs.com/">Stakeholder Labs</a><span> builds tools for modern organizations that want to up level their shareholder analytics and engagement to support long-term growth. </span><a href="https://4hh9sba6ifb.typeform.com/to/QLkJgVAe?typeform-">Contact us</a><span> to learn more.</span></strong></p><div class="subscription-widget-wrap"><div class="subscription-widget show-subscribe"><div class="preamble"><p>Want more IR marketing insights in your inbox? IROs can build trust and clarity with every update.\",\"body_html\":\"<p>When it comes to communicating with investors, public companies have a powerful tool at their disposal: the shareholder letter. Often included in an annual report\u2014but increasingly distributed quarterly\u2014a shareholder letter isn\u2019t just a summary of financial results. It's a strategic narrative that can build trust, align expectations, and even reinforce a company\u2019s brand.&nbsp;</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link Letter?</strong></h2><p>A shareholder letter is a direct communication from a company\u2019s leadership\u2014usually the CEO or chairman\u2014to its shareholders. The primary purpose is to provide an overview of the company\u2019s performance over the past quarter or year. But beyond the numbers, these letters serve as a platform for leaders to articulate their vision, explain strategic decisions, and build a narrative that resonates with investors.</p><p>Historically, shareholder letters were simple recaps of financial performance. But as the role of investor relations has evolved, so too have shareholder letters. They have become a key component within the investor marketing toolkit.</p><h2><strong>How Shareholder Letters Support Investor Marketing Goals</strong></h2><p>Investor marketing is all about building a strong brand around your company and stock ticker. Modern investors expect more frequent communication, in formats they find accessible and easy to digest. They are also looking for strong signals of competent and trustworthy leadership\u2014and these letters are one of the things investors look at to read between the lines.</p><div href=\\\"https://www.marketwatch.com/story/how-sharp-investors-use-a-ceos-annual-shareholder-letter-as-a-secret-door-to-superior-stocks-2020-11-06\\\">Image via MarketWatch</a></figcaption></figure></div><p>A well-crafted shareholder letter should do three things:</p><ul><li><p><strong>Educate Investors: </strong>Clearly explain the company\u2019s performance, strategy, and market conditions in a way that\u2019s accessible to all investors, regardless of their financial expertise.&nbsp;</p></li><li><p><strong>Build Trust: </strong>Be candid about successes and challenges. Transparency fosters trust, and trust is crucial for maintaining investor confidence. Effective shareholder letters demonstrate thought leadership in the category and a confidence in leadership to meet its stated objectives.</p></li><li><p><strong>Align Expectations: </strong>Set realistic expectations for the future by clearly outlining the company\u2019s long-term strategy and goals.</p></li></ul><h2><strong>Key Elements of an Effective Shareholder Letter</strong></h2><p>Shareholder letters vary by company and executives; however, there are common attributes that many of them follow to meet their stated objectives. These aren\u2019t just best practices\u2014they\u2019re must-haves for making a letter that truly connects with investors and boosts your ticker brand.</p><h3><strong>1. Clarity and simplicity</strong></h3><p>The best shareholder letters are easy to read. They avoid jargon and complex language. Instead, they focus on clear, direct communication. The goal is to make sure every investor understands the company\u2019s performance and strategy without needing a financial dictionary.</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a company\u2019s mission and focus.</figcaption></figure></div><h3><strong>2. Transparency and candor</strong></h3><p>Investors appreciate honesty. If the company faced challenges during the year, don\u2019t gloss over them. Address them directly, explain the steps being taken to overcome them, and be transparent about what this means for the future. This honesty not only builds credibility but also sets realistic expectations for the future.</p><p>Consider this example from Block CEO Jack Dorsey, which walks investors through with candid remarks on the parts of the company\u2019s he and the team are focused on \u201Cfixing.\u201D</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 is-viewable-img\\\" href=\\\"https://s22.q4cdn.com/959853165/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/FINAL-Q2-24-Shareholder-Letter.pdf\\\">Netflix Shareholder Letter</a></figcaption></figure></div><h3><strong>3. Engagement through storytelling</strong></h3><p>A shareholder letter should tell a story. This could be the story of the company\u2019s growth, a reflection on the year\u2019s major events, or an insight into the company\u2019s long-term vision. Storytelling makes the letter more engaging and helps investors connect with the company on a more personal level. Warren Buffett\u2019s letters to Berkshire Hathaway shareholders are a prime example\u2014they are as famous for their folksy, engaging style as they are for their financial insights.</p><p>We\u2019re seeing companies utilize the real estate afforded by shareholder letters to add additional layers of storytelling, including social proof from customer testimonials and media coverage. </p><p>Consider this example from Block, which pulls publicly-shared tweets from customers within its letter.</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 is-viewable-img\\\" This is effective since shareholders may not see every headline on their own. In aggregate, the media recap spotlights public reception and signals company momentum.</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 is-viewable-img\\\" href=\\\"https://s24.q4cdn.com/723050407/files/doc_earnings/2024/q2/presentation/Zillow-2Q24-Shareholders-Letter.pdf\\\">Zillow Shareholder Letter</a> provides context to a key leadership update</figcaption></figure></div><h3><strong>4. Focus on long-term strategy</strong></h3><p>While it\u2019s important to discuss the past quarter\u2019s performance, a shareholder letter should also focus on the future. What is the company\u2019s long-term strategy? How does management plan to achieve it? This forward-looking approach helps to align investor expectations with the company\u2019s goals.&nbsp;</p><p>When talking about key themes that will drive the company in the future, there is no such thing as overemphasizing. These statements should be repeated over and over again so that investors have a clear sense of where you\u2019re going.</p><p>In this example from Rivian, the beginning of the letter opens with a succinct breakdown of the quarter\u2019s highlights and priorities. Using this format, the key points can be digested at-a-glance.</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 is-viewable-img\\\" target=\\\"_blank\\\" company\u2019s top priorities</figcaption></figure></div><h3><strong>5. Use of visuals</strong></h3><p>Incorporating charts, tables, and other visuals can help to break down complex information and make the letter more accessible. Historically, this has meant using data visualization to bring financial statement trends to life. Increasingly, companies are also working in product screenshots and other creative elements that help bring the story to life.</p><p>Personalization is a major focus for Hims &amp; Hers, so the company shows momentum in subscribers utilizing personalized products as a key visual in its quarterly letter.</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 href=\\\"https://s25.q4cdn.com/442043304/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/Snap-Inc-Q2-2024-Investor-Letter.pdf\\\">Snap Shareholder Newsletter</a></figcaption></figure></div><h2><strong>The Evolving Nature of Shareholder Letters</strong></h2><p>Over time, the format and content of shareholder letters have evolved. Where once these letters were little more than dry financial recaps, they are now dynamic documents that reflect the company\u2019s brand and values. In the past shareholder letters usually came once per year, but now, many companies are opting to distribute them monthly alongside quarterly earnings reports.</p><h3><strong>Improved distribution and accessibility</strong></h3><p>Companies are also improving their approach to distribution. While most companies host shareholder letters on their IR website, others push shareholders to sign up for quarterly newsletter alerts direct to their inbox. An example is <a href=\\\"https://investor.snap.com/overview/default.aspx\\\">Snap</a>, which sends out a quarterly investor newsletter inclusive of IR and product updates. </p><p>In the example below, <a href=\\\"https://www.siemens.com/global/en/company/investor-relations/investor-services.html\\\">Siemens</a> includes a strong call to action to \u201Csubscribe\u201D alongside its quarterly letter. </p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 is-viewable-img\\\" target=\\\"_blank\\\" Design</strong></h3><p>Plain, text-heavy documents might work for Warren Buffett, but Berkshire Hathaway is the exception and not the rule in this case. href=\\\"https://www.berkshirehathaway.com/letters/letters.html\\\">Berkshire Hathaway</a>\u2019s repository of past letters</figcaption></figure></div><p>Most IROs will want to look to companies like <a href=\\\"https://investors.duolingo.com/\\\">Duolingo</a>, which have integrated vibrant visuals and product-related stories into their shareholder letters, making them not only informative but also visually appealing. Other examples include <a href=\\\"https://downloads.rivian.com/2md5qhoeajym/1TJ6otGQ8dmSyIi0shstum/3f443d04053237a398665ae3dd4a79dd/EX_-_99.2_2Q24_Shareholder_Letter_Final.pdf\\\">Rivian</a>, <a href=\\\"https://s27.q4cdn.com/787306631/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/FINAL-Q2-2024-Shareholder-Letter_Linked-1.pdf\\\">Airbnb</a>, <a href=\\\"https://investors.sonder.com/static-files/414a6101-2aa0-4716-ba03-8c1610606c52\\\">Sonder</a>, <a href=\\\"https://s27.q4cdn.com/787306631/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/FINAL-Q2-2024-Shareholder-Letter_Linked-1.pdf\\\">Hims &amp; Hers</a>, and <a href=\\\"https://s29.q4cdn.com/628966176/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/Block_Shareholder-Letter_2Q24.pdf\\\">Block</a>, to name a few.</p><p>The big challenge when upleveling the design of your IR materials? Resources.</p><p>IR teams do not typically sit within the same area of the organization as marketing and creative teams do, and often do not have the same access to these resources in their own work streams. But this is all changing, as many companies that have recently gone public are clearly giving IR teams the creative resources they need.</p><p>The example from Airbnb almost looks like a print advertisement, but it\u2019s actually just a well-designed page from a recent shareholder letter.</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 Branding Opportunities</strong></h3><p>Shareholder letters are a great way for executives to <a href=\\\"https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/p/c-suite-branding-the-key-to-connecting\\\">show who they are as leaders</a>. The tone and delivery of these letters give investors a sense of what the leader values and how they approach their role. It\u2019s not just about company updates\u2014it\u2019s also about letting their personality and leadership style shine through.</p><p>For example, <a href=\\\"https://s21.q4cdn.com/198919461/files/doc_financials/2024/q2/Shareholder-Letter-Q2-24-FINAL.pdf\\\">Morningstar\u2019s CEO includes a reading list</a> to give shareholders a sense of the topics he\u2019s following, and what\u2019s sparking his curiosity as of late.</p><div class=\\\"captioned-image-container\\\"><figure><a class=\\\"image-link image2 is-viewable-img\\\" shareholder letter does more than report on a company\u2019s financials\u2014it serves as a critical tool in investor marketing.</strong> By focusing on clarity, transparency, and storytelling, companies can create shareholder letters that not only inform but also engage and inspire. As investor expectations continue to evolve, so too must the way companies communicate.&nbsp;</p><p>By mastering the art of the shareholder letter, companies can turn a simple communication into a powerful tool for building investor confidence and driving long-term growth.</p><p><strong><a href=\\\"https://stakeholderlabs.com/\\\">Stakeholder Labs</a> builds tools for modern organizations that want to up level their shareholder analytics and engagement to support long-term growth. <a href=\\\"https://4hh9sba6ifb.typeform.com/to/QLkJgVAe?typeform-\\\">Contact us</a> to learn more.</strong></p><div class=\\\"subscription-widget-wrap-editor\\\" data-attrs=\\\"{&quot;url&quot;:&quot;https://blog.stakeholderlabs.com/subscribe?&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;Subscribe&quot;,&quot;language&quot;:&quot;en&quot;}\\\" data-component-name=\\\"SubscribeWidgetToDOM\\\"><div class=\\\"subscription-widget show-subscribe\\\"><div class=\\\"preamble\\\"><p class=\\\"cta-caption\\\">Want more IR marketing insights in your inbox? Sign up for updates.</p></div><form class=\\\"subscription-widget-subscribe\\\"><input type=\\\"email\\\" class=\\\"email-input\\\" name=\\\"email\\\" placeholder=\\\"Type your email\u2026\\\" tabindex=\\\"-1\\\"><input type=\\\"submit\\\" class=\\\"button primary\\\" value=\\\"Subscribe\\\"><div class=\\\"fake-input-wrapper\\\"><div class=\\\"fake-input\\\"></div><div class=\\\"fake-button\\\"></div></div></form></div></div>\",\"truncated_body_text\":\"When it comes to communicating with investors, public companies have a powerful tool at their disposal: the shareholder letter. Often included in an annual report\u2014but increasingly distributed quarterly\u2014a shareholder letter isn\u2019t just a summary of financial results. It's a strategic narrative that can build trust, align expectations, and even reinforce
